**Andrea Kimi Antonelli** (Italian: [\[anˈdrɛːa ˈkiːmi antoˈnɛlli\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Italian); born 25 August 2006) is an Italian [racing driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_driver) who competes in [Formula One](10854.md) for [Mercedes](25098794.md). Antonelli has won eight [Formula One Grands Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_Grands_Prix) since his debut in [2025](72768531.md).

Born and raised in [Bologna](21069333.md), Antonelli is the son of [sportscar racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportscar_racing) driver [Marco Antonelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Antonelli). After a successful [karting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting) career—culminating in back-to-back victories at the [senior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_Kart) [European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_European_Championship) in 2020 and 2021—Antonelli graduated to [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae). He won his first titles in [Italian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_F4_Championship) and [ADAC F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_ADAC_Formula_4_Championship) in 2022 with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing); in addition to becoming a race-winner in [Italian GT3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_GT_Championship), he also won a [gold medal](526488.md) for [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletes_at_the_2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games#Team_Italy) at the [FIA Motorsport Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games_Formula_4_Cup). He then claimed both the [Formula Regional European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_Regional_European_Championship) and [Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_Regional_Middle_East_Championship) Championships before progressing to [FIA Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2) in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Formula_2_Championship), winning two races as he finished sixth.

A member of the [Mercedes Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Junior_Team) since 2019,[^cite_note-MJTnote-1] Antonelli signed for [Mercedes](25098794.md) in [2025](72768531.md), replacing [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) to partner [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)) and becoming the [third-youngest driver in Formula One history](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_start_a_race) at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Grand_Prix), aged 18; he achieved his maiden podium finish in his rookie season at the [Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Canadian_Grand_Prix) and became the [youngest driver to set a fastest lap](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_set_a_fastest_lap). Mercedes emerged as the lead contender in [2026](71681684.md), with Antonelli winning eight Grands Prix—including [his home Grand Prix in Italy](81006624.md)—while becoming the [youngest-ever polesitter](13599687.md#Youngest_polesitters) in [China](80284337.md) and the [youngest World Drivers' Championship leader](13599687.md#Championships,_other) in [Japan](80284355.md).

As of the [2026 Bahrain Grand Prix](80201648.md), Antonelli has achieved eight race wins, six pole positions, ten fastest laps, and fifteen podiums in Formula One. In addition to the accolades above, he holds several [Formula One records](13599687.md), including being the [youngest driver to lead a lap](13599687.md#Led_for_at_least_one_lap,_youngest_leaders) and [to score a *grand chelem*](13599687.md#Other). Antonelli is contracted to remain at Mercedes until at least the end of the 2027 season.

## Early life

Marco Antonelli driving the Mercedes-AMG GT3 Evo in the 2022 International GT Open at the Red Bull Ring

Antonelli is the son of [sportscar racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportscar_racing) driver and team owner [Marco Antonelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Antonelli).

Andrea Kimi Antonelli was born on 25 August 2006 in [Bologna](21069333.md), Emilia-Romagna, Italy.[^cite_note-MJT-2] His father, [Marco Antonelli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Antonelli), is a [sportscar racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportscar_racing) driver and owner of the [San Marino](27248.md)–based *AKM Motorsport* racing team, which has competed in the [Italian F4 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_F4_Championship) since [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_F4_Championship).[^cite_note-3][^cite_note-4] He founded *Antonelli Motorsport* in 1993, which competed in several [GT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_tourer) series, winning the [2018 Italian GT Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Italian_GT_Championship).[^cite_note-5][^cite_note-6][^cite_note-7] His mother, Veronica Pomaro, is a former employee of [Poste Italiane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poste_Italiane) and has worked in [motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorsport) since 1997, helping Marco operate AKM.[^cite_note-8][^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10] He has a younger sister named Maggie.[^cite_note-11]

Antonelli shares his middle name with the forename of [Kimi Räikkönen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimi_R%C3%A4ikk%C3%B6nen)—the [2007 Formula One World Drivers' Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Formula_One_World_Championship); Antonelli has stated that the name was given by [Enrico Bertaggia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrico_Bertaggia) to his father, who wanted his son to have a foreign name after "Andrea", and that he was not named after Räikkönen.[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-TG2024-13] He studied [international relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_relations) and [marketing](59252.md) at *[ITCS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istituto_tecnico_economico) Gaetano Salvemini* in [Casalecchio di Reno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casalecchio_di_Reno) and learned to speak English during his race weekends, as well as German upon joining the [Mercedes Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Junior_Team).[^cite_note-14][^cite_note-TK2021-15] His father initially wanted him to play [football](10568.md) in Italy before entering him into [karting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting), as he showed more passion for motorsport.[^cite_note-16] He grew up idolising three-time World Drivers' Champion [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna) and would watch [DVD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DVD) footage of his triumphs with his father; he also idolised Argentine footballer [Lionel Messi](2150841.md).[^cite_note-17][^cite_note-18] He first drove a [Lamborghini Super Trofeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamborghini_Super_Trofeo) at the age of nine, with his father operating the pedals.[^cite_note-TK2021-15]

## Junior racing career

### Karting (2014–2021)

#### 2014–2017: Career beginnings and international debut

Antonelli was introduced to [kart racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racing) through his father and, aged five, began driving at Galliano Park in [Forlì](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forl%C3%AC).[^cite_note-SEY2025-19] He started competing two years later and won championships in several categories of the discipline.[^cite_note-MS2024-20] After attending a [summer camp](926264.md#Sports_camps) with the [Automobile Club d'Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile_Club_d'Italia) in 2014, Antonelli was [scouted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scout_\(sport\)) by Giovanni Minardi—the son of [Giancarlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giancarlo_Minardi)—who signed him to *Minardi Management*.[^cite_note-ACI2022-21] He moved to [Birel ART](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birel_ART)'s *Easykart* [spec series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spec_series) in 2015, where he won the Trofeo Italia in the 60 [cc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Engine_displacement) class, as well as the International Grand Final from twenty-first on the [grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G); he also reached the final of the [WSK Final Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Final_Cup) in [60 Mini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60_Mini).[^cite_note-TK2021-15][^cite_note-MS2024-20][^cite_note-22] Antonelli entered several [major competitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kart_racing_championships) with [Tony Kart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Kart) in 2016, achieving [podium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Podium#In_motorsport) finishes in the Italian Cup and Trofeo delle Industrie, as well as the [WSK Super Master Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Super_Master_Series) in 2017.[^cite_note-DriverDB-23][^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] He won the third round of the [Italian Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Karting_Championship) that year at [Battipaglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battipaglia).[^cite_note-26]

#### 2018–2019: International success as a Mercedes Junior

Signing to the [Mercedes Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Junior_Team) in 2018,[^cite_note-MJTnote-1] Antonelli joined Energy Corse and won the [WSK Champions Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Champions_Cup), South Garda Winter Cup, and ROK Cup International Final in the mini classes that year, as well as finishing runner-up to [Martinius Stenshorne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinius_Stenshorne) in the Italian Championship.[^cite_note-TK2021-15][^cite_note-29] Antonelli progressed to [OK-Junior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OK-Junior)—the premier under-15 international karting [category](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIK-FIA#Categories)—in 2019, winning six [international titles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_kart_racing_champions): the South Garda Winter Cup, WSK Super Master Series, [WSK Euro Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Euro_Series), WSK Open Cup, and [WSK Final Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WSK_Final_Cup), as well as his first Italian Championship, all with [Rosberg Racing Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nico_Rosberg).[^cite_note-TK2021-15][^cite_note-MS2024-20][^cite_note-30] He entered his first [CIK-FIA European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_European_Championship) and [World Championships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship) that year, finishing runner-up to [Marcus Amand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Amand) at the former after winning the final round at [Le Mans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_la_Sarthe) and fifth at the latter.[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32] He closed his junior karting career [in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_kart_racing_championships#United_States), where he was runner-up to [Brent Crews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brent_Crews) at the [SKUSA SuperNationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SKUSA_SuperNationals) in KA100 Junior and won both junior titles in the SKUSA Winter Series with a perfect record.[^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34]

#### 2020–2021: Dominance in senior classes

In 2020, Antonelli progressed to the senior [Original Kart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_Kart) (OK) class with Kart Republic, aged 13.[^cite_note-TK2021-15] In addition to finishing third at the South Garda Winter Cup and runner-up to [Nikita Bedrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikita_Bedrin) at the WSK Super Master Series, he won the WSK Euro Series and Italian Championship.[^cite_note-TK2021-15][^cite_note-35][^cite_note-36] As a rookie, he became the youngest senior [European Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_European_Championship#European_Champions) in history, two weeks after turning 14.[^cite_note-TK2021-15][^cite_note-FIAKROTY-37] Antonelli retired from the final of the World Championship at [Portimão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algarve_International_Circuit) with an injury; he collided with the stationary kart of [Maya Weug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maya_Weug) in wet-weather conditions, suffering a broken left [tibia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tibia) and [metatarsus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metatarsus).[^cite_note-FIAKROTY-37][^cite_note-38] For his efforts in 2020, Antonelli was named "Rookie of the Year" by the [Commission Internationale de Karting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_Internationale_de_Karting) (CIK-FIA).[^cite_note-FIAKROTY-37]

Antonelli moved to the professional [KZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KZ_\(karting\)) gearbox class upon his return in 2021.[^cite_note-RBR21-39][^cite_note-VK2021-40] He struggled with [braking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Braking) throughout the year due to his leg injuries.[^cite_note-RBR21-39] Bidding to become the youngest-ever [KZ World Champion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karting_World_Championship#Gearbox_class_\(1983%E2%80%932000,_2013%E2%80%93present\))—a record held by [Max Verstappen](41758713.md) since 2013—he finished fifteenth after taking [pole position](630713.md) for the [heats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#H).[^cite_note-41][^cite_note-42][^cite_note-VK2021-40] He further took pole as a [guest driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G) at [Adria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adria) in the KZ European Championship.[^cite_note-43] He successfully defended his OK European Championship, dominating the season with five wins from eight rounds, and finished runner-up to [Rafael Câmara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_C%C3%A2mara) at the WSK Champions Cup and WSK Super Master Series.[^cite_note-44][^cite_note-45][^cite_note-46][^cite_note-47] Upon his graduation to [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae), *Vroomkart* opined that Antonelli's karting career "already \[foretold a] classic anthology of a champion".[^cite_note-VK2021-40]

### Formula 4 (2021–2022)

#### 2021: Junior formulae debut

Antonelli driving the Tatuus F4-T014 in the 2021 Italian F4 Championship at the Red Bull Ring

Antonelli debuted in the [2021 Italian F4 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_F4_Championship) at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring).

Three weeks after he turned 15, Antonelli made his [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae) debut in the fifth round of the [2021 Italian F4 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Italian_F4_Championship), driving for [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Powerteam) at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring).[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49] He had tested [Formula 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_4) (F4) machinery at the circuit with his father's [AKM Motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AKM_Motorsport) team in March, whilst recovering from his broken leg, as well as at [Vallelunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallelunga_Circuit) and a five-day [Ferrari Driver Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrari_Driver_Academy) scouting camp at [Fiorano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiorano_Circuit), which he was nominated for by the [Automobile Club d'Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automobile_Club_d'Italia) (ACI).[^cite_note-RBR21-39] On debut, he finished ninth—the highest-placed rookie—in his first race and scored four points across the weekend.[^cite_note-RBR21-39] Antonelli scored further points in the following round at [Mugello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugello_Circuit), before his breakout weekend in the final event at [Monza](952083.md).[^cite_note-50] He finished second in the opener after a race-long battle with champion-elect [Oliver Bearman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Bearman) and scored additional podiums in both remaining races.[^cite_note-51][^cite_note-52] Antonelli finished tenth in the overall standings—having contested three of seven rounds—and fourth in the rookies' championship with four wins in the category.[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-ACI2022-21][^cite_note-54]

#### 2022: Maiden championships and gold medal

Antonelli driving the Tatuus F4-T421 out of the pitlane at Spa-Francorchamps in the 2022 ADAC F4 Championship

Antonelli dominated [Italian F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_F4) in [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_F4_Championship) with a then-record 13 victories.

In preparation for his primary campaigns with Prema in 2022, Antonelli contested select rounds of the [F4 UAE Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Formula_4_UAE_Championship), now signed by the ACI as a member of its "[Team Italia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACI_Sport)" by virtue of winning the *Supercorso Federale* in November 2021.[^cite_note-22UAE-55][^cite_note-ACI2022-21][^cite_note-56] He finished third after qualifying on [pole position](630713.md) for the [non-championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#N) trophy round, held in support of the [Abu Dhabi Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-57] He was originally set to only contest round three of the main championship, but a [COVID-19](63030231.md) diagnosis for karting rival [Rafael Câmara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_C%C3%A2mara) saw Antonelli replace him in the season-opener, where he took three consecutive on-track victories, demoted to third in the latter following a post-race penalty.[^cite_note-22UAE-55][^cite_note-22UAER1-58][^cite_note-59][^cite_note-60] Another podium in the [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) final race saw him lead the championship by 34 points; he did not contest the second round, as planned.[^cite_note-61][^cite_note-62][^cite_note-22UAER1-58] Accumulating another podium in the final race of round three at [Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Autodrome)—again being penalised from victory—Antonelli ended eighth in the standings after contesting two of five rounds.[^cite_note-63][^cite_note-64] He remained in [Italian F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Italian_F4_Championship) for the first of his main campaigns, taking pole for the [Imola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imola_Circuit) season-opener before retiring with a [gearbox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gearbox) issue.[^cite_note-65][^cite_note-66] He received [front wing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downforce) damage after clipping a kerb in race two, and was penalised for a collision with teammate [James Wharton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Wharton_\(racing_driver\)) in the final race which demoted him from fourth to tenth.[^cite_note-67] His fortunes changed with two wins in the next round at [Misano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misano_World_Circuit), followed by six consecutive victories at [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa-Francorchamps) and [Vallelunga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vallelunga_Circuit)—where he took three *[grands chelems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_chelem)*—which propelled him to lead the standings ahead of Câmara.[^cite_note-Gascoigne-2022-68] Further wins at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring) and [Monza](952083.md) consolidated his championship advantage, with his streak of seven consecutive victories ended by a puncture, prior to clinching the title with victory in the first race of the season-ending [Mugello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugello_Circuit) round.[^cite_note-Gascoigne-2022-68] He won the remaining two races, ending the season on a then-record 13 victories as a rookie.[^cite_note-69][^cite_note-70][^cite_note-Gascoigne-2022-68]

Antonelli lifting his trophy after winning at Spa-Francorchamps in the 2022 ADAC F4 Championship, between Rafael Câmara and Charlie Wurz

Antonelli further claimed the [ADAC F4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_ADAC_Formula_4_Championship) title, followed by a [gold medal](526488.md) at the [FIA Motorsport Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games_Formula_4_Cup).

In parallel with his Italian programme, Antonelli also competed in the Germany-based [ADAC F4 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_ADAC_Formula_4_Championship).[^cite_note-71] He seized two victories in the first round at Spa-Francorchamps, before taking all available wins, poles, and [fastest laps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fastest_lap) at the [Hockenheimring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hockenheimring).[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] He added two further consecutive wins at [Zandvoort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Zandvoort) before missing out on another clean-sweep [by 0.058 seconds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photo_finish) to [Conrad Laursen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conrad_Laursen) in the [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) race.[^cite_note-Gascoigne-2022-68] After Prema skipped the penultimate event at the [Lausitzring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lausitzring), Antonelli clinched the championship with one race to spare, having taken victory in the first two races during a rain-affected final round at the [Nürburgring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C3%BCrburgring).[^cite_note-Gascoigne-2022-68][^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75] Following his maiden titles, the ACI selected Antonelli to represent [Team Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athletes_at_the_2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games#Team_Italy) in the [F4 Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games_Formula_4_Cup) of the biennial [FIA Motorsport Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games) at [Paul Ricard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Paul_Ricard).[^cite_note-76] After securing pole by over half-a-second to [Charlie Wurz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlie_Wurz), he won both the qualifying and main races whilst driving with a broken left wrist—owing to a collision near the end of qualifying—to take the [gold medal](526488.md) and secure Italy's victory in the [overall medal table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIA_Motorsport_Games#Medal_table).[^cite_note-MG2022-77][^cite_note-FS2022-78][^cite_note-79] He stated that he experienced "a lot of pain" and could not drive properly, requiring [painkillers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painkiller) to continue.[^cite_note-80] He subsequently received a [€](9472.md)10,000 grant from [chassis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chassis) manufacturer [KC Motorgroup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KC_Motorgroup) to support his 2023 campaigns.[^cite_note-81] Antonelli graduated from F4 with 26 wins, 24 pole positions, and 37 podium finishes in 57 appearances, becoming the second-most successful driver in the history of the discipline.[^cite_note-82] Roger Gascoigne of *Formula Scout* opined that Antonelli was "in a class of his own" as he received the highest driver awards from the ACI and *[Autosprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corriere_dello_Sport)*.[^cite_note-Gascoigne-2022-68]

### Formula Regional (2023)

Antonelli driving the Tatuus FR-19 at the Red Bull Ring in the 2023 Formula Regional European Championship

Antonelli won both the [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_Regional_European_Championship) and [Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_Regional_Middle_East_Championship) Championships in his first season of [Formula Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Regional).

Prior to his main 2023 campaign in [Formula Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Regional), Antonelli contested the [Middle East Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_Regional_Middle_East_Championship) with [Mumbai Falcons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mumbai_Falcons).[^cite_note-83] He opened the season with a trio of second-placed finishes across the opening two rounds, propelling him to the championship lead.[^cite_note-84] He consolidated his advantage with three wins at the third and fourth rounds in [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Motor_Town) and [Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Autodrome), respectively.[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86] He led title rival [Taylor Barnard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taylor_Barnard) of [PHM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PHM_Racing) by 37 points going into the final round at [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit), where he received a penalty for colliding with Barnard whilst battling for the lead in race one.[^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] Antonelli secured the title with a race to spare, as well as his seventh podium in the final race.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90] He then progressed to the [European Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Formula_Regional_European_Championship), remaining with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing) alongside [Lorenzo Fluxá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorenzo_Flux%C3%A1) and career rival [Rafael Câmara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_C%C3%A2mara).[^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92] Antonelli expressed that he was still "lacking a bit of experience", so progression to [FIA Formula 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3) was "a bit early for \[him]".[^cite_note-FS2022-78] Prior to the season, Marcus Simmons of *[Autosport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autosport)* compared the intrigue surrounding his campaign to the [Formula Three](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Three) (F3) debuts of [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna), [Mika Häkkinen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mika_H%C3%A4kkinen), and [Max Verstappen](41758713.md), whilst Salvo Sardina of *[La Gazzetta dello Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Gazzetta_dello_Sport)* described him as a "predestinato" (lit. '[predestined](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Predestined)').[^cite_note-93][^cite_note-GdS2023-28] His season started with a second-place at [Imola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imola_Circuit), repeating this feat in both races at [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya).[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] A [gear selection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gearbox) issue denied his first victory at the [Hungaroring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungaroring), before finally clinching victory in the second race at [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa-Francorchamps), which was marred by the fatal accident of [Dilano van 't Hoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dilano_van_'t_Hoff).[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98]

Antonelli driving the Tatuus FR-19 at the Hockenheimring in the 2023 Formula Regional European Championship, with Rafael Câmara in the background

Antonelli became the first European to win multiple [F3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_Three)-level championships in a year since [Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost) in 1979.

Additional wins from pole position at [Mugello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mugello_Circuit) and [Paul Ricard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Paul_Ricard) followed for Antonelli, who became the championship leader into the summer break, ahead of [Martinius Stenshorne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martinius_Stenshorne).[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100] He earned another podium at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring) following a penalty for Stenshorne, before achieving two on-track victories at [Monza](952083.md); he was stripped of the first win for illegal use of the [push-to-pass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Push-to-pass) system.[^cite_note-101][^cite_note-MNZ2023-102][^cite_note-103] Upon his dominant victory in the second race at [Zandvoort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_Zandvoort)—winning by over 12 seconds in mixed-weather conditions after starting eighth—Antonelli clinched the championship, becoming the first European to win multiple F3-level championships in a year since [Alain Prost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alain_Prost) in [1979](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_1979_motorsport_champions).[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105][^cite_note-106] He later described the win as "one of \[his] best races".[^cite_note-107] Antonelli dedicated his title to Van 't Hoff and was voted driver of the season by the team principals.[^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109] He ended the season with five wins from 11 podiums and four pole positions, finishing 39 points ahead of Stenshorne and 61 points ahead of [Tim Tramnitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Tramnitz).[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111] *Formula Scout* ranked Antonelli as the best driver in junior formulae throughout 2023.[^cite_note-112]

### FIA Formula 2 (2024)

Antonelli qualifying for the 2024 Spielberg Formula 2 round

Antonelli (pictured in [Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Spielberg_Formula_2_round)) progressed to [FIA Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2) with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing) in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Formula_2_Championship).

Antonelli bypassed the international [FIA Formula 3 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_3_Championship), progressing directly to [FIA Formula 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2) in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Formula_2_Championship) with [Prema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prema_Racing), alongside [Ferrari Driver Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrari_Driver_Academy) member [Oliver Bearman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Bearman).[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114] Ahead of the season, Antonelli remarked that he "\[did not] want to set any expectation  \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*]" amidst early rumours of his Formula One prospects for [2025](72768531.md).[^cite_note-115] Prema struggled to adapt to [new regulations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallara_F2_2024) utilising [ground effect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_effect_\(cars\)),[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-TR2024-117] with Antonelli claiming a single point at the season-opener in [Sakhir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Sakhir_Formula_2_round) after finishing fourteenth in the [sprint race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship#Race_weekend) and tenth in the [feature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Formula_2_Championship#Race_weekend), ahead of Bearman in both races.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119] He secured a pair of sixth-placed finishes the following round in [Jeddah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Jeddah_Formula_2_round).[^cite_note-120] After qualifying second in [Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Melbourne_Formula_2_round), he spun into the gravel whilst making up places in the [reverse-grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#R) sprint,[^cite_note-122] before battling with [Dennis Hauger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Hauger) for the lead of the feature, where he ultimately finished fourth.[^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] Antonelli again finished fourth in both the [Imola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Imola_Formula_2_round) feature and [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Monte_Carlo_Formula_2_round) sprint, ending the latter weekend seventh in the feature.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129] After placing outside of the points in [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Barcelona_Formula_2_round) and [Spielberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Spielberg_Formula_2_round), Antonelli sat ninth in the standings halfway through the season; [Toto Wolff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toto_Wolff) commented that [Mercedes](25098794.md) were unfazed by his results and that "a champion needs to be thrown in the cold water".[^cite_note-TR2024-117][^cite_note-130]

Antonelli leads the sprint race of the 2024 Silverstone Formula 2 round on "extreme wet" tyres

Antonelli took his maiden F2 victory at the rain-affected [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Silverstone_Formula_2_round) round.

Antonelli qualified tenth at [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Silverstone_Formula_2_round), putting him on pole for the rain-affected sprint.[^cite_note-131] After three [safety cars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_car) and a [red flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_flag_\(motorsport\)), Antonelli secured his maiden win and podium in his eighth F2 round.[^cite_note-132][^cite_note-133][^cite_note-134] He retired from the feature, following a first-lap collision with [Kush Maini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kush_Maini).[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] In [Budapest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Budapest_Formula_2_round), a gamble on [option tyres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#O) saw him lead the majority of the sprint, but forced him to make an unscheduled [pit stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop) after a loss of tyre performance.[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138] The following day, Antonelli took his first feature race victory after starting seventh on the grid, elevating him to sixth in the standings.[^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140] He finished sixth in the curtailed [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Spa-Francorchamps_Formula_2_round) sprint after an award-winning overtake on [Franco Colapinto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franco_Colapinto) into *[Eau Rouge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Spa-Francorchamps#Eau_Rouge_and_Raidillon)*,[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-DA2024-142] before finishing ninth in the feature following a slow pit stop; he then received a ten-place grid penalty—for Prema's usage of [dry ice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dry_ice) as a [cooling agent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooling_agent)—which applied at the [Monza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Monza_Formula_2_round) sprint, where he was involved in a multi-car collision.[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] He placed fourth in the feature, following a contentious battle with teammate Bearman.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] Antonelli missed out on his maiden [pole position](630713.md) by seventeen-thousandths of a second in [Baku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Baku_Formula_2_round), finishing seventh in the sprint and third in the feature, after contact with [Zane Maloney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zane_Maloney) in the latter.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151] He retired from both races in [Lusail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lusail_Formula_2_round), subsequent to a collision with Maini in the sprint and [Richard Verschoor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Verschoor) in the feature, the latter released unsafely into his path in the [pit lane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_lane).[^cite_note-152][^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154] Antonelli withdrew from the season-ending round at [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Yas_Island_Formula_2_round) with illness, finishing the season sixth in the standings on 113 points with two victories from three podiums, 38 ahead of teammate Bearman in twelfth.[^cite_note-SF2024-155][^cite_note-156]

## Formula One career

In April 2019, [Mercedes](25098794.md) announced that Antonelli had joined its [Junior Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_Junior_Team) at the age of 12.[^cite_note-MJTnote-1] In a move contrasted to that of [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) to [McLaren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McLaren_Driver_Development_Programme) in 1998,[^cite_note-ilM2020-157][^cite_note-158][^cite_note-ES2018-159] he had rejected preliminary advances from the [Ferrari Driver Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferrari_Driver_Academy), which he was too young to join.[^cite_note-160] On 17–18 April 2024, Antonelli conducted his first two-day test with the team, driving the [W12](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_W12) at the [Red Bull Ring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring).[^cite_note-161][^cite_note-162] He completed further private tests in the [W13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_W13) at [Imola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imola_Circuit), [Silverstone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silverstone_Circuit), [Barcelona-Catalunya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Barcelona-Catalunya), and [Spa-Francorchamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spa-Francorchamps).[^cite_note-163] Following a new rule that re-allowed drivers to hold an [FIA Super Licence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIA_Super_Licence) at the minimum age of 17, Antonelli was rumoured to replace [Logan Sargeant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Logan_Sargeant) at [Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Williams_Racing) midway through the [2024](72504121.md) season; Mercedes denied the move, instead recommending [Mick Schumacher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mick_Schumacher) for the position.[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167]

Antonelli made his [free practice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_practice) debut at the [2024 Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Italian_Grand_Prix), taking part in the first session in [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\))'s [W15](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_W15).[^cite_note-168] He set the fastest lap before he spun at the [*Curva Alboreto*](952083.md) corner 10 minutes into the session, colliding with the tyre barrier at 52 [*g*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G-force) (510 [m/s<sup>2</sup>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metre_per_second_squared); 1,700 [ft/s<sup>2</sup>](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foot_per_second_squared)).[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171][^cite_note-TG2024-13] [Toto Wolff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toto_Wolff) defended Antonelli after the crash, whilst [Mark Hughes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Hughes_\(journalist\)) of *[Motor Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motor_Sport_\(magazine\))* compared his free practice debut to that of [Tazio Nuvolari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tazio_Nuvolari).[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173] Antonelli entered another free practice session at the [Mexico City Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix), where he finished twelfth after again ending his session prematurely—sustaining floor damage whilst running over a piece of debris.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] He featured alongside Russell in the post-season test at [Yas Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit), completing 62 laps and setting the fifth-fastest time overall.[^cite_note-176] He then completed a two-day test in the [W11](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_F1_W11_EQ_Performance) at [Jerez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuito_de_Jerez).[^cite_note-177]

### Mercedes (2025–present)

#### 2025: Podiums in rookie season

Antonelli driving the Mercedes W16 at the 2025 Japanese Grand Prix

Antonelli (pictured at the [Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix)) debuted in Formula One with [Mercedes](25098794.md) in [2025](72768531.md), aged 18.

Antonelli signed for [Mercedes](25098794.md) in [2025](72768531.md), replacing [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) to partner [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)) on a one-year contract.[^cite_note-2025contract-178] With his debut at the [Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Australian_Grand_Prix), he became the [first Italian driver](37543516.md) to compete in Formula One since [Antonio Giovinazzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antonio_Giovinazzi) in [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Abu_Dhabi_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180] the first Mercedes rookie since [1954](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1954_Formula_One_season),[^cite_note-181] as well as the [third-youngest driver in Formula One history](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_start_a_race) at 18 years and 203 days old;[^cite_note-2025contract-178] he qualified sixteenth with floor damage before climbing to fourth in the rain-affected Grand Prix,[^cite_note-182] making several on-track overtakes and an opportune [pit stop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_stop) for [intermediate tyres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermediate_tyres) to become the [second-youngest points-scorer in Formula One history](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_score_points) and the highest-placed debutant since [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Australian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184] After finishing seventh on his [sprint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_racing#Sprint) debut in [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Chinese_Grand_Prix), he clinched sixth in the main race after disqualifications for both [Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari) drivers, having sustained floor damage again due to debris.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] He qualified and finished sixth in [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix), where he became both the youngest driver [to lead a race](13599687.md#Led_for_at_least_one_lap,_youngest_leaders) and [to set a fastest lap](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_set_a_fastest_lap) in Formula One history.[^cite_note-JPN25-187] He also became the third driver to achieve three consecutive top-six finishes from debut, after [Jackie Stewart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jackie_Stewart) ([1965](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1965_Formula_One_season)) and [Lewis Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewis_Hamilton) ([2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Formula_One_World_Championship)).[^cite_note-NYT2025-188] Antonelli qualified fourth for the [Bahrain Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Bahrain_Grand_Prix)—demoted to fifth with a [grid penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G) for a team error—before dropping outside the [points](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_World_Championship_points_scoring_systems) on a three-stop strategy, finishing eleventh.[^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190] He finished sixth in [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Saudi_Arabian_Grand_Prix) amidst tyre wear concerns with the [W16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_W16).[^cite_note-191]

On [pole position](630713.md) for the [Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Miami_Grand_Prix) sprint,[^cite_note-MIA25-192] Antonelli dropped to seventh in wet conditions after a first-corner incident with [Oscar Piastri](59449712.md) and a [pit lane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pit_lane) collision with [Max Verstappen](41758713.md);[^cite_note-193] he qualified third and finished sixth in the main race following an ill-timed [virtual safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_safety_car).[^cite_note-194] Suspension upgrades for his local [Emilia Romagna Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Emilia_Romagna_Grand_Prix) contributed to high-speed instability,[^cite_note-195] where he qualified thirteenth before retiring with a [throttle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Throttle) issue whilst running in eighth.[^cite_note-196] A barrier collision at the *[Nouvelle Chicane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_de_Monaco)* in [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Monaco_Grand_Prix) saw him qualify fifteenth, before ending up last of the finishers on an alternate strategy.[^cite_note-197] He again retired from the [Spanish Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Spanish_Grand_Prix) due to an [oil pressure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_pressure) loss in seventh.[^cite_note-198] By finishing third at the [Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Canadian_Grand_Prix), holding off championship-leader Piastri, Antonelli became the [third-youngest podium finisher in Formula One history](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_score_a_podium_finish) as teammate Russell won.[^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] He crashed into Verstappen on the first lap in [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Austrian_Grand_Prix) at *[Ams Ag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Bull_Ring)*, [locking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skid_\(automobile\)) his rear [brakes](65423.md) and forcing both drivers' retirements;[^cite_note-201] he subsequently received a three-place grid penalty for the [British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_British_Grand_Prix), where he started tenth before being rear-ended by [Isack Hadjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isack_Hadjar) and retiring with [diffuser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diffuser_\(automotive\)) damage.[^cite_note-202] Seventeenth in the [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Belgian_Grand_Prix) sprint, he qualified eighteenth for the Grand Prix, where he climbed to sixteenth after starting in the pit lane.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204]

Antonelli driving the Mercedes W16 at the 2025 Italian Grand Prix

Antonelli (pictured at the [Italian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Italian_Grand_Prix)) achieved three podiums and became the [highest-scoring rookie in Formula One history](13599687.md#Points,_other).

Mercedes reverted to their old rear suspension for the [Hungarian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) in a bid to boost Antonelli's confidence,[^cite_note-205] where he finished tenth after starting fifteenth following a [track limits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#T) violation in qualifying.[^cite_note-206] He climbed from eleventh to sixth at the [Dutch Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Dutch_Grand_Prix) before receiving back-to-back penalties for a collision with [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) and speeding in the pit lane, demoting him to sixteenth.[^cite_note-207] At his first [home Grand Prix in Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Italian_Grand_Prix), he qualified seventh—0.043 seconds behind Russell—before a slow start dropped him to tenth; initially recovering to eighth, he received a penalty for forcing [Alexander Albon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Albon) off-track and lost a position;[^cite_note-208] [Toto Wolff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toto_Wolff) described his performance as "underwhelming".[^cite_note-209] He qualified fourth in [Azerbaijan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix),[^cite_note-210] where he battled and passed [Liam Lawson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liam_Lawson) for third before being [overcut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#O) by Russell.[^cite_note-211] Mercedes rebounded in [Singapore](77743466.md), where Antonelli finished fifth from fourth on the [grid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G) as Russell won.[^cite_note-212] Eighth in the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_United_States_Grand_Prix) sprint, he qualified seventh for the Grand Prix before [Carlos Sainz Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Sainz_Jr.) collided with and dropped him outside the points.[^cite_note-213] Starting sixth in [Mexico City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Mexico_City_Grand_Prix), he climbed to fourth in the opening laps before being [ordered to cede position](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_orders) to Russell as a counter to the advancing Piastri, who—alongside Verstappen—later passed both in the [pit window](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#P) and Russell returned sixth to Antonelli.[^cite_note-214] He clinched second in the [São Paulo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) sprint in a race-long battle for the lead with [Lando Norris](48978813.md), prior to his maiden [front-row start](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#F) in the Grand Prix, where he held off Verstappen in the closing laps for another second-place;[^cite_note-215] Luke Slater of *[The Daily Telegraph](23797577.md)* praised his rookie-season improvements and noted it as "the first weekend where he thoroughly out-performed Russell".[^cite_note-216] Heavy rain in [Las Vegas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Las_Vegas_Grand_Prix) saw Antonelli qualify seventeenth, before a second-lap stop for [hard-compound tyres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_tyres) elevated him to fourth, fending off Piastri and Leclerc on fresh tyres; he initially dropped to fifth with a [false start](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_start) penalty, but claimed another podium after Piastri and Norris were disqualified.[^cite_note-217] Sixth in the [Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Qatar_Grand_Prix) sprint, Antonelli finished fifth in the Grand Prix after passing Russell and conceding fourth to championship-leader Norris in the closing laps;[^cite_note-218] he received [death threats](13262036.md) on social media upon unfounded speculation by [Red Bull](1173193.md) that he let Norris past.[^cite_note-219] In [Abu Dhabi](77743503.md), he qualified fourteenth and finished in fifteenth.[^cite_note-220]

#### 2026: Rise to prominence

Antonelli standing on top of his car with his fist in the air after winning the 2026 Chinese Grand Prix

Antonelli became the [youngest polesitter](13599687.md#Youngest_polesitters) before taking his maiden victory at the [Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md) in [2026](71681684.md).

Antonelli was confirmed to remain at Mercedes, alongside [George Russell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Russell_\(racing_driver\)), for their [2026](71681684.md) "masterplan".[^cite_note-2026contract-221] Widely expected by insiders and [bookmakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bookmaker) to be a contender amidst [new power unit and chassis regulations](71681684.md#Regulation_changes), Mercedes emerged as the leading constructor over [Ferrari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scuderia_Ferrari) with a superior [energy recovery system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_recovery_system) on the [W17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes_W17).[^cite_note-222][^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225] In [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Australian_Grand_Prix), a battery-hampered start dropped Antonelli from second to seventh before he caught the lead trio and capitalised on a Ferrari strategy miscue to complete a [1–2 finish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1%E2%80%932_finish) for Mercedes.[^cite_note-226] Following a fifth-place in the [China](80284337.md) sprint ensuing a slow start and a penalty for colliding with [Isack Hadjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isack_Hadjar), Antonelli became the [youngest polesitter in Formula One history](13599687.md#Youngest_polesitters), eclipsing [Sebastian Vettel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebastian_Vettel) by nearly two years; he withstood an early Ferrari charge and won the main race by five seconds over Russell, further becoming the [second-youngest Grand Prix winner](13599687.md#Youngest_winners) and the first Italian victor [in two decades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Malaysian_Grand_Prix).[^cite_note-CHN26-227][^cite_note-228] Another slow start dropped him from pole to sixth in [Japan](80284355.md), after which he capitalised on a well-timed [safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safety_car) to lead and pulled a 13-second margin over [Oscar Piastri](59449712.md) to become the [youngest World Drivers' Championship leader](13599687.md#Championships,_other).[^cite_note-JPN26-229] Sixth in the [Miami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Miami_Grand_Prix) sprint, Antonelli held off [Lando Norris](48978813.md) to win the Grand Prix—becoming the first driver in history to convert their first three poles—and extended his lead over Russell to 20 points.[^cite_note-230] Antonelli finished third in the [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Canadian_Grand_Prix) sprint amidst a contentious battle for the lead with Russell, which they continued into the Grand Prix, where Antonelli emerged victorious after several excursions for both drivers and Russell's engine failure.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

Antonelli standing on top of his car with his fist in the air after winning the 2026 Chinese Grand Prix

Antonelli became the [youngest World Drivers' Championship leader](13599687.md#Championships,_other) and led by 50 points at the summer break.

Mercedes entered [Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Monaco_Grand_Prix) challenged by Ferrari and [Red Bull](1173193.md), with Russell further attempting [mind games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mind_games) in the media to quell Antonelli's momentum.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234] Antonelli set an acclaimed "statement" lap to snatch pole from [Max Verstappen](41758713.md),[^cite_note-235][^cite_note-236][^cite_note-237] dominating the Grand Prix to become the [youngest driver](13599687.md#Other) to score a *[grand chelem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_chelem)* and to [win in Monaco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monaco_Grand_Prix#Winners), as Russell fell outside the points and 68 behind Antonelli.[^cite_note-MON26-238][^cite_note-239] His run of five consecutive victories—the [most from a maiden win in history](13599687.md#Wins,_other)—was halted in [Barcelona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Barcelona-Catalunya_Grand_Prix), retiring with a late engine failure immediately after passing Russell for second.[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-REU2026-241] His lead dropped to 25 points after he finished third in a mistake-laden weekend in [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Austrian_Grand_Prix) and fifteenth after a mechanical issue in [Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_British_Grand_Prix) prevented a late pass on [Charles Leclerc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Leclerc) for the lead, having become the [youngest-ever sprint winner](13599687.md#Sprints,_other) and taken pole the day prior.[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243][^cite_note-R\&T2026-244] Antonelli won the [Belgian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Belgian_Grand_Prix) from pole, withstanding Leclerc and capitalising on a Russell–Hamilton collision to re-open his points advantage.[^cite_note-245] As [McLaren](20994.md) rejuvenated in the mid-season, third- and second-placed finishes for Antonelli in [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Hungarian_Grand_Prix) and the [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Dutch_Grand_Prix)—both ahead of Russell—extended his lead to 59 points ahead of [his home Grand Prix in Italy](81006624.md), where Mercedes opted for him to claim a new [power unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_engines) at the cost of a heavy [grid penalty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glossary_of_motorsport_terms#G).[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] Starting nineteenth, Antonelli climbed to twelfth prior to a third-lap [red flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_flag_\(motorsport\)), fifth by lap 10, and third on lap 13, where he joined Russell and Verstappen in a lead trio; having passed Verstappen, he engaged in an 11-lap battle with Russell, until a [virtual safety car](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_safety_car) saw Antonelli pit from his [less durable tyres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formula_One_tyres).[^cite_note-249] He subsequently gained over 15 seconds in 21 laps to pass Russell and become the first Italian victor of the Grand Prix [in 60 years](1122358.md), doing so from the [second-lowest victorious grid position in history](13599687.md#Wins_from_farthest_back_on_the_starting_grid).[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251] He won the first [Spanish Grand Prix](79774929.md) at the [Madring](80044756.md), but lost out in qualifying for the following [Azerbaijan Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Azerbaijan_Grand_Prix) after making a mistake and started sixteenth. He survived two safety car restarts and made his way up the field to finish fifth.

#### 2027

Ahead of the [2026 British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_British_Grand_Prix), Antonelli was announced to be retained by Mercedes alongside Russell for 2027.[^cite_note-252]

## Other racing

Marco Antonelli driving the Mercedes-AMG GT3 Evo in the 2022 International GT Open at the Red Bull Ring

Antonelli won his debut [sportscar race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportscar_race) at the [2023 Italian GT3 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_GT_Championship), driving the pictured [Mercedes-AMG GT3 Evo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_GT3_Evo).

In May 2023, Antonelli debuted in [sportscar racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportscar_racing), competing for his father's [AKM Motorsport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AKM_Motorsport) outfit in the opening round of the [Italian GT3 Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Italian_GT_Championship) at [Misano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misano_World_Circuit), driving the [Mercedes-AMG GT3 Evo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_GT3_Evo).[^cite_note-ACI2022-21] He won the first race from pole position, before securing third-place in race two with a final-corner overtake on eventual champion [Jens Klingmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Klingmann).[^cite_note-253][^cite_note-254] Antonelli tested the GT3 Evo at [Imola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imola_Circuit) the following year.[^cite_note-255]

Antonelli was initially set to take part in a [Super Formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Formula) post-season test in December 2024, driving the [Dallara SF23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallara_SF23) alongside Prema teammate [Oliver Bearman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Bearman) at [Suzuka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzuka_Circuit); he later withdrew from the test due to illness.[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-SF2024-155] In 2026, he served as a [test driver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_driver) for the [Mercedes-AMG GT2 Edition W16](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_GT2_Edition_W16), which featured his signature on the doorframes.[^cite_note-257]

## Other ventures

Antonelli appeared in a cameo role as Matteo in the [sports drama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_drama) film *[Italian Race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Race)*[^cite_note-258] (2016), which won four awards at the [Nastro d'Argento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nastro_d'Argento).[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] He co-founded the [kart racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racing) team *AKM Motorsport by Kart Republic* alongside his father in 2023, where he serves as a driver coach and chassis tester.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] He is the subject of a short [documentary film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Documentary_film), *[The Seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Seat_\(film\))* (2025), directed by [Kyle Thrash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyle_Thrash) and distributed by [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix), which documented his promotion to [Mercedes](25098794.md) for [2025](72768531.md).[^cite_note-263] He was appointed the ambassador of Italian sport for [San Marino](27248.md) by the [Congress of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_State) in 2025 and served as a [torchbearer](74217.md) ahead of the [2026 Winter Olympics](38416091.md).[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265]

## Personal life

Antonelli smiling in a white Mercedes hat

Antonelli at the [2026 Australian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Australian_Grand_Prix)

Antonelli selected the number 12 as his [personal driver number](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Formula_One_driver_numbers) in Formula One, citing inspiration from his idol, [Ayrton Senna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayrton_Senna); he also used the number throughout his 2022 and 2023 title-winning seasons in [junior formulae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junior_formulae).[^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] He passed his [driving test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Driving_test) six weeks before his Formula One debut and continued studying online for his *[maturità](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matura)* through the first half of [his rookie season](72768531.md).[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269][^cite_note-NYT2025-188] Antonelli was given a [GT 63 S](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_GT#Second_generation_\(C192\)) by [Mercedes-AMG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG) that year, but is unable to drive it in Italy for three years as its [power-to-weight ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power-to-weight_ratio) exceeds national limits for new drivers six-fold.[^cite_note-270] He received a limited-edition [GT 63 PRO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercedes-AMG_GT#Mercedes-AMG_GT_63_Pro_\(2025\)) in January 2026 and crashed it a month later near his home in [Serravalle, San Marino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serravalle,_San_Marino); he escaped unharmed.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272]

From 2023 to 2026, Antonelli was in a relationship with Czech [kart racer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kart_racer) Eliška Bábíčková, who won the [OK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Original_Kart) class of the [Italian Karting Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Karting_Championship) in the former year and is the sister of [2022 W Series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_W_Series) driver Tereza Bábíčková—who competed against him in karts.[^cite_note-273][^cite_note-TEK15-274][^cite_note-275] His [racing helmet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racing_helmet) uses a base colour of [Savoy blue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savoy_blue), with green, white, and red details, inspired by the [national colours of Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_colours_of_Italy).[^cite_note-276] He has stated that he aspires to reach the success of fellow Italian sportsmen [Jannik Sinner](60061043.md) and [Valentino Rossi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valentino_Rossi).[^cite_note-277] Outside of motor racing, he supports the local [association football](10568.md) and [basketball](3921.md) clubs [Bologna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bologna_FC_1909) and [Virtus Bologna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtus_Bologna), respectively.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-279] He has also expressed interest in pursuing [automotive design](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automotive_design) and [architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture).[^cite_note-SEY2025-19]

#### Formula One records

Antonelli holds the following [Formula One records](13599687.md):

| Record | Achieved | Ref |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Youngest driver to lead a lap](13599687.md#Led_for_at_least_one_lap,_youngest_leaders) | 18 years, 225 days | [2025 Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-JPN25-187] |
| [Youngest driver to set a fastest lap](13599687.md#Youngest_drivers_to_set_a_fastest_lap) | 18 years, 225 days | [2025 Japanese Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Japanese_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-JPN25-187] |
| [Youngest sprint polesitter](13599687.md#Sprints,_other) | 18 years, 250 days | [2025 Miami Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Miami_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-MIA25-192] |
| [Youngest driver to score a sprint podium finish](13599687.md#Sprints,_other) | 19 years, 75 days | [2025 São Paulo Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_S%C3%A3o_Paulo_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-307] |
| [Most points in first championship season](13599687.md#Points,_other) | 150 | [2025](72768531.md) | [^cite_note-308] |
| [Youngest polesitter](13599687.md#Youngest_polesitters) | 19 years, 202 days | [2026 Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md) | [^cite_note-CHN26-227] |
| [Youngest driver to win from pole position](13599687.md#Other) | 19 years, 202 days | [2026 Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md) | [^cite_note-CHN26-2-309] |
| [Youngest driver to score a hat-trick](13599687.md#Other) | 19 years, 202 days | [2026 Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md) | [^cite_note-CHN26-2-309] |
| [Youngest World Drivers' Championship leader](13599687.md#Championships,_other) | 19 years, 216 days | [2026 Japanese Grand Prix](80284355.md) | [^cite_note-JPN26-229] |
| [Youngest driver to win consecutive Grands Prix](13599687.md#Wins,_other) | 19 years, 216 days | [2026 Japanese Grand Prix](80284355.md) | [^cite_note-310] |
| [Youngest driver to set a sprint fastest lap](13599687.md#Sprints,_other) | 19 years, 271 days | [2026 Canadian Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Canadian_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-311] |
| [Youngest driver to score a *grand chelem*](13599687.md#Other) | 19 years, 286 days | [2026 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Monaco_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-MON26-238] |
| [Most consecutive wins from maiden win](13599687.md#Wins,_other) | 5 | [2026 Chinese Grand Prix](80284337.md) – [2026 Monaco Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Monaco_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-REU2026-241] |
| [Youngest sprint winner](13599687.md#Sprints,_other) | 19 years, 313 days | [2026 British Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_British_Grand_Prix) | [^cite_note-R\&T2026-244] |
